# Paper 1

## S03-Q001

Domain 1 · Assessment and prediction

Which airway assessment test has been shown to predict ease of laryngoscopy and intubation better than the Mallampati score?

A. The upper-lip-bite test is a better predictor than Mallampati score for ease of laryngoscopy and intubation.

B. A beard can make mask sealing difficult.

C. Normal neck extension should be 35 degrees or more.

D. Limited mouth opening predicts difficult blade insertion and tongue displacement.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: A**

**A — correct:** The upper-lip-bite test is a better predictor than Mallampati score for ease of laryngoscopy and intubation.

The upper-lip-bite test is a better predictor than Mallampati score for ease of laryngoscopy and intubation.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Recognize upper-lip-bite and mandibular-protrusion findings that predict laryngoscopy difficulty.

**B:** A beard can make mask sealing difficult.

A beard can make mask sealing difficult.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “A beard can make mask sealing difficult.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize upper-lip-bite and mandibular-protrusion findings that predict laryngoscopy difficulty.

**C:** Normal neck extension should be 35 degrees or more.

Normal neck extension should be 35 degrees or more.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Normal neck extension should be 35 degrees or more.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize upper-lip-bite and mandibular-protrusion findings that predict laryngoscopy difficulty.

**D:** Limited mouth opening predicts difficult blade insertion and tongue displacement.

Limited mouth opening predicts difficult blade insertion and tongue displacement.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Limited mouth opening predicts difficult blade insertion and tongue displacement.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize upper-lip-bite and mandibular-protrusion findings that predict laryngoscopy difficulty.

**Evidence sources:**

- Chestnut 6e (2019).pdf — PDF p. 765, passage 273630
- Chestnut 6e (2019).pdf — PDF p. 765, passage 273629
- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 2302, passage 247157

## S03-Q003

Domain 1 · Preoxygenation and mask ventilation

Integrate supine CPAP and postinduction PEEP to extend nonhypoxemic apnea in obesity. Which statement is best supported?

A. Supine 10 cm H2O CPAP raises postintubation PaO2 and decreases atelectasis.

B. Obese patients reach the desaturation endpoint in less than 3 minutes after induction.

C. Combining CPAP during preoxygenation with PEEP/mechanical ventilation after induction can increase nonhypoxemic apnea duration from 2 to 3 minutes.

D. Supine 7.5 cm H2O CPAP during 3 minutes of preoxygenation did not change time to desaturation to SpO2 90%.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Supine 10 cm H2O CPAP raises postintubation PaO2 and decreases atelectasis.

Supine 10 cm H2O CPAP raises postintubation PaO2 and decreases atelectasis.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Supine 10 cm H2O CPAP raises postintubation PaO2 and decreases atelectasis.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Integrate supine CPAP and postinduction PEEP to extend nonhypoxemic apnea in obesity.

**B:** Obese patients reach the desaturation endpoint in less than 3 minutes after induction.

Obese patients reach the desaturation endpoint in less than 3 minutes after induction.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Obese patients reach the desaturation endpoint in less than 3 minutes after induction.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Integrate supine CPAP and postinduction PEEP to extend nonhypoxemic apnea in obesity.

**C — correct:** Combining CPAP during preoxygenation with PEEP/mechanical ventilation after induction can increase nonhypoxemic apnea duration from 2 to 3 minutes.

Combining CPAP during preoxygenation with PEEP/mechanical ventilation after induction can increase nonhypoxemic apnea duration from 2 to 3 minutes.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Integrate supine CPAP and postinduction PEEP to extend nonhypoxemic apnea in obesity.

**D:** Supine 7.5 cm H2O CPAP during 3 minutes of preoxygenation did not change time to desaturation to SpO2 90%.

Supine 7.5 cm H2O CPAP during 3 minutes of preoxygenation did not change time to desaturation to SpO2 90%.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Supine 7.5 cm H2O CPAP during 3 minutes of preoxygenation did not change time to desaturation to SpO2 90%.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Integrate supine CPAP and postinduction PEEP to extend nonhypoxemic apnea in obesity.

**Evidence sources:**

## S03-Q004

Domain 1 · Preoxygenation and mask ventilation

During preoxygenation, which positioning change improves preoxygenation quality in both obese and nonobese patients?

A. Head-down positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.

B. Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in nonobese patients.

C. Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in obese patients.

D. Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: D**

**A:** Head-down positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.

Head-up positioning improves preoxygenation in obese patients.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Head-down positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize head-up positioning as an intervention that improves preoxygenation quality.

**B:** Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in nonobese patients.

Head-up positioning is the supported positional intervention described for improving preoxygenation.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in nonobese patients.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize head-up positioning as an intervention that improves preoxygenation quality.

**C:** Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in obese patients.

Head-up positioning improves preoxygenation in nonobese patients.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation only in obese patients.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize head-up positioning as an intervention that improves preoxygenation quality.

**D — correct:** Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.

Head-up positioning improves the quality of preoxygenation in both obese and nonobese patients.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Recognize head-up positioning as an intervention that improves preoxygenation quality.

## S03-Q005

Domain 1 · Airway devices and intubation

According to the Difficult Airway Society 2025 guidelines for management of unanticipated difficult tracheal intubation in adults, after videolaryngoscopic intubation, what two-point check confirms tracheal placement?

A. Confirm intubation with sustained waveform exhaled CO2 and visualization of the tube through the vocal cords.

B. Use external laryngeal manipulation when the glottic view is poor.

C. A hyperangulated videolaryngoscope may be paired with a stylet, bougie, or flexible bronchoscope.

D. When laryngoscopy or tracheal intubation is difficult, continue oxygenation and optimize position, blade, adjuncts, tube, suction, and neuromuscular block.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: A**

Confirm intubation with sustained waveform exhaled CO2 and visualization of the tube through the vocal cords.

Use external laryngeal manipulation when the glottic view is poor.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Use external laryngeal manipulation when the glottic view is poor.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply a current difficult-airway confirmation strategy after videolaryngoscopic intubation.

**C:** A hyperangulated videolaryngoscope may be paired with a stylet, bougie, or flexible bronchoscope.

A hyperangulated videolaryngoscope may be paired with a stylet, bougie, or flexible bronchoscope.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “A hyperangulated videolaryngoscope may be paired with a stylet, bougie, or flexible bronchoscope.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply a current difficult-airway confirmation strategy after videolaryngoscopic intubation.

**D:** When laryngoscopy or tracheal intubation is difficult, continue oxygenation and optimize position, blade, adjuncts, tube, suction, and neuromuscular block.

When laryngoscopy or tracheal intubation is difficult, continue oxygenation and optimize position, blade, adjuncts, tube, suction, and neuromuscular block.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “When laryngoscopy or tracheal intubation is difficult, continue oxygenation and optimize position, blade, adjuncts, tube, suction, and neuromuscular block.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply a current difficult-airway confirmation strategy after videolaryngoscopic intubation.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Systolic blood pressure can approximate afterload when aortic stenosis is absent.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply preload and afterload definitions when interpreting ventricular loading.

**D:** Afterload is the systolic load on the left ventricle after contraction begins.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Afterload is the systolic load on the left ventricle after contraction begins.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply preload and afterload definitions when interpreting ventricular loading.

Blood viscosity usually has a minor effect on flow.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Blood viscosity usually has a minor effect on flow.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply radius-dependent resistance when interpreting vascular blood-flow changes.

**C:** Blood flow through a vascular bed is inversely related to resistance.

Blood flow through a vascular bed is inversely related to resistance.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Blood flow through a vascular bed is inversely related to resistance.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply radius-dependent resistance when interpreting vascular blood-flow changes.

**D — correct:** Small changes in vessel caliber produce large resistance changes because resistance varies with the fourth power of vessel radius.

Small changes in vessel caliber produce large resistance changes because resistance varies with the fourth power of vessel radius.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Apply radius-dependent resistance when interpreting vascular blood-flow changes.

## S03-Q016

Domain 2 · Ischemic heart disease and perioperative risk

If surgery cannot be delayed and P2Y12 interruption is mandatory after coronary stenting, which antiplatelet plan is best supported?

A. Consider surgery after 3 months when further delay is riskier.

B. Delay elective surgery according to stent type and timing.

C. If surgery requires stopping a P2Y12 inhibitor after coronary stenting, continue aspirin when possible and restart the P2Y12 inhibitor promptly after surgery.

D. Use a consensus decision to balance surgical and antiplatelet risks.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Consider surgery after 3 months when further delay is riskier.

After drug-eluting stent placement, elective surgery may be considered after 3 months if further delay carries greater risk.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Consider surgery after 3 months when further delay is riskier.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply antiplatelet continuation after coronary-stent placement when P2Y12 interruption is required.

**B:** Delay elective surgery according to stent type and timing.

Elective noncardiac surgery should be delayed 30 days after bare-metal stent and ideally 6 months after drug-eluting stent placement.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Delay elective surgery according to stent type and timing.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply antiplatelet continuation after coronary-stent placement when P2Y12 interruption is required.

**C — correct:** If surgery requires stopping a P2Y12 inhibitor after coronary stenting, continue aspirin when possible and restart the P2Y12 inhibitor promptly after surgery.

If surgery requires stopping a P2Y12 inhibitor after coronary stenting, continue aspirin when possible and restart the P2Y12 inhibitor promptly after surgery.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Apply antiplatelet continuation after coronary-stent placement when P2Y12 interruption is required.

**D:** Use a consensus decision to balance surgical and antiplatelet risks.

A consensus decision among treating clinicians can help weigh surgical risk against discontinuing or continuing antiplatelet agents.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Use a consensus decision to balance surgical and antiplatelet risks.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply antiplatelet continuation after coronary-stent placement when P2Y12 interruption is required.

**Evidence sources:**

- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 3399, passage 249081
- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 3394, passage 249072
- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 1795, passage 246332

## S03-Q017

Domain 2 · Ischemic heart disease and perioperative risk

Recognize aspirin-withdrawal risk as a reason for continuation in selected high-risk patients. Which statement is best supported?

A. Aspirin irreversibly inhibits platelet COX-1.

B. Use aspirin selectively when cardiac risk exceeds bleeding risk.

C. Continue perioperative statin therapy.

D. Aspirin withdrawal syndrome has been associated with acute perioperative cardiovascular events.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: D**

**A:** Aspirin irreversibly inhibits platelet COX-1.

Aspirin is an irreversible platelet COX-1 inhibitor that decreases aggregation and thrombotic risk.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Aspirin irreversibly inhibits platelet COX-1.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize aspirin-withdrawal risk as a reason for continuation in selected high-risk patients.

**B:** Use aspirin selectively when cardiac risk exceeds bleeding risk.

Aspirin may be appropriate in selected populations when cardiac-event risk exceeds major-bleeding risk.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Use aspirin selectively when cardiac risk exceeds bleeding risk.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize aspirin-withdrawal risk as a reason for continuation in selected high-risk patients.

**C:** Continue perioperative statin therapy.

Patients taking statins should generally continue them during the perioperative period.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Continue perioperative statin therapy.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize aspirin-withdrawal risk as a reason for continuation in selected high-risk patients.

**D — correct:** Aspirin withdrawal syndrome has been associated with acute perioperative cardiovascular events.

Aspirin withdrawal syndrome has been associated with acute perioperative cardiovascular events.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Recognize aspirin-withdrawal risk as a reason for continuation in selected high-risk patients.

**Evidence sources:**

- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 3394, passage 249072
- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 1795, passage 246332

## S03-Q020

Domain 2 · Heart failure and cardiomyopathy

Which drug classes are identified as primary outpatient therapy for pediatric hypertrophic cardiomyopathy?

A. Diuretics are not indicated and can be detrimental by reducing left-ventricular volume and increasing outflow-tract obstruction.

B. Pediatric therapies can include longitudinal observation, medical management, an implantable cardioverter-defibrillator, surgery, or transplantation.

C. Beta-blockers and calcium-channel blockers are the primary outpatient drugs.

D. Drugs that augment myocardial contractility, such as inotropic agents and calcium infusions, are not well tolerated.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Diuretics are not indicated and can be detrimental by reducing left-ventricular volume and increasing outflow-tract obstruction.

## S03-Q040

Recognize the cerebral blood-flow threshold at which EEG deterioration usually begins during ischemia monitoring. Which statement is best supported?

A. Frank cellular failure appears to occur below approximately 10 to 12 mL/min/100 g brain tissue.

B. Cerebral blood-flow decreases as low as approximately 22 mL/min/100 g may be tolerated without EEG changes.

C. Normal cerebral blood flow is approximately 50 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

D. EEG deterioration usually begins when cerebral blood flow falls below approximately 15 to 18 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: D**

**B:** Cerebral blood-flow decreases as low as approximately 22 mL/min/100 g may be tolerated without EEG changes.

Cerebral blood-flow decreases as low as approximately 22 mL/min/100 g may be tolerated without EEG changes.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Cerebral blood-flow decreases as low as approximately 22 mL/min/100 g may be tolerated without EEG changes.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize the cerebral blood-flow threshold at which EEG deterioration usually begins during ischemia monitoring.

**C:** Normal cerebral blood flow is approximately 50 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

Normal cerebral blood flow is approximately 50 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Normal cerebral blood flow is approximately 50 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize the cerebral blood-flow threshold at which EEG deterioration usually begins during ischemia monitoring.

**D — correct:** EEG deterioration usually begins when cerebral blood flow falls below approximately 15 to 18 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

EEG deterioration usually begins when cerebral blood flow falls below approximately 15 to 18 mL/min/100 g of brain tissue.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Recognize the cerebral blood-flow threshold at which EEG deterioration usually begins during ischemia monitoring.

## S03-Q096

Domain 7 · Maternal physiology and pathology

Which comparison correctly describes gastric emptying outside labor versus during labor?

A. It is significantly delayed both outside labor and during labor.

B. It remains unchanged both outside labor and during labor.

C. It is delayed outside labor but returns toward normal during labor.

D. It is not significantly delayed outside labor but is delayed during labor.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: D**

**A:** It is significantly delayed both outside labor and during labor.

The cited comparison does not show significant delay in nonlaboring patients; delay develops during labor.

**B:** It remains unchanged both outside labor and during labor.

Gastric emptying is delayed during labor, so it does not remain unchanged across both states.

**C:** It is delayed outside labor but returns toward normal during labor.

This reverses the documented relationship: delay is not significant outside labor but occurs during labor.

**D — correct:** It is not significantly delayed outside labor but is delayed during labor.

This is the best answer because it states the documented comparison between nonlaboring pregnancy and labor.

**Evidence sources:**

- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 3478, passage 249225
- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 3477, passage 249224

## S03-Q099

Domain 7 · Labor analgesia

Which statement integrates both the analgesic-efficacy limitation and the dose-limiting adverse-effect problem of an opioid-only labor epidural?

A. Fentanyl or sufentanil is typically added to labor epidural local anesthetic to reduce local-anesthetic administration.

B. Epidural alpha-1 receptor activation delays vascular uptake of local anesthetic and opioid.

C. An opioid-only epidural regimen does not provide adequate analgesia without unacceptable side effects.

D. Pruritus is the most troublesome complication limiting epidural fentanyl and sufentanil dose.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Fentanyl or sufentanil is typically added to labor epidural local anesthetic to reduce local-anesthetic administration.

Adding fentanyl or sufentanil to local anesthetic reduces local-anesthetic administration, but this describes the combined regimen rather than both limitations of opioid-only treatment.

**B:** Epidural alpha-1 receptor activation delays vascular uptake of local anesthetic and opioid.

Delayed vascular uptake through epidural alpha-1 receptor activation is a pharmacologic mechanism, not the combined efficacy-and-toxicity limitation requested.

**C — correct:** An opioid-only epidural regimen does not provide adequate analgesia without unacceptable side effects.

An opioid-only epidural fails to provide adequate analgesia without unacceptable adverse effects; this is the only option integrating both efficacy and dose-limiting toxicity.

**D:** Pruritus is the most troublesome complication limiting epidural fentanyl and sufentanil dose.

Pruritus is an important dose-limiting adverse effect, but it addresses toxicity alone and not the inadequate-analgesia component of the question.

**Evidence sources:**

- Miller 10e (2024).pdf — PDF p. 2259, passage 291177
- Miller 10e (2024).pdf — PDF p. 2259, passage 291176

## S03-Q101

When epidural fentanyl or sufentanil is added for labor analgesia, which adjustment to the local-anesthetic regimen is supported?

**D:** Increase the concentration while continuing the same long-acting local anesthetic.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Increase the concentration while continuing the same long-acting local anesthetic.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Explain why epidural fentanyl or sufentanil is combined with dilute local anesthetic during labor analgesia.

A patient develops dyspnea, confusion, and petechiae after a long-bone fracture. What diagnosis and timing are characteristic?

A. Bone cement implantation syndrome includes hypoxia from increased pulmonary shunt.

B. DVT and pulmonary embolism can complicate pelvic and lower-extremity orthopedic operations.

C. Fat embolism syndrome within 72 hours after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

D. Bone cement implantation syndrome may cause heart block and sinus arrest.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Bone cement implantation syndrome includes hypoxia from increased pulmonary shunt.

Bone cement implantation syndrome includes hypoxia from increased pulmonary shunt.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Bone cement implantation syndrome includes hypoxia from increased pulmonary shunt.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Identify the characteristic presentation and timing of fat embolism syndrome after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “DVT and pulmonary embolism can complicate pelvic and lower-extremity orthopedic operations.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Identify the characteristic presentation and timing of fat embolism syndrome after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

**C — correct:** Fat embolism syndrome within 72 hours after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

Fat embolism syndrome classically occurs within 72 hours after long-bone or pelvic fracture and presents with dyspnea, confusion, and petechiae.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Identify the characteristic presentation and timing of fat embolism syndrome after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

**D:** Bone cement implantation syndrome may cause heart block and sinus arrest.

Bone cement implantation syndrome may cause heart block and sinus arrest.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Bone cement implantation syndrome may cause heart block and sinus arrest.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Identify the characteristic presentation and timing of fat embolism syndrome after long-bone or pelvic fracture.

- Lange 7e (2022).pdf — PDF p. 1498, passage 278660

## S03-Q117

A trauma patient with possible cervical-spine injury requires emergency intubation. Which approach is recommended?

## S03-Q237

Domain 14 · Ophthalmic anesthesia

An awake patient develops circumoral tingling shortly after a regional anesthetic. What does this represent?

A. Circumoral paresthesia is followed by lightheadedness, dizziness, and visual or auditory disturbances.

B. Circumoral paresthesia is the earliest sign of local-anesthetic toxicity in an awake patient; under general anesthesia early CNS toxicity may be missed.

C. Objective CNS-toxicity signs include shivering, slurred speech, and muscle twitching.

D. Increasing local-anesthetic levels can progress from CNS excitation and seizures to respiratory depression and arrest.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: B**

**A:** Circumoral paresthesia is followed by lightheadedness, dizziness, and visual or auditory disturbances.

The development of circumoral paresthesia is followed by prodromal CNS symptoms of lightheadedness and dizziness, which progress to both visual and auditory disturbances, such as difficulty in focusing and tinnitus.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Circumoral paresthesia is followed by lightheadedness, dizziness, and visual or auditory disturbances.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize circumoral paresthesia as the earliest sign of systemic local-anesthetic toxicity.

**B — correct:** Circumoral paresthesia is the earliest sign of local-anesthetic toxicity in an awake patient; under general anesthesia early CNS toxicity may be missed.

The key clinical principle is that only 7 cases among 112,000 pediatric regional anesthetics, the majority of which occurred after caudal blocks.109 Under general anesthesia the early recognition of CNS toxicity may not be apparent until devastating cardiovascular effects occur.110 In awake patients, the earliest sign of local anesthetic toxicity is circumoral paresthesia, which is the result of the high tissue concentrations of local anesthetic rather than CNS effects.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Recognize circumoral paresthesia as the earliest sign of systemic local-anesthetic toxicity.

**C:** Objective CNS-toxicity signs include shivering, slurred speech, and muscle twitching.

Objective signs of CNS toxicity are shivering, slurred speech, and muscle twitching.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Objective CNS-toxicity signs include shivering, slurred speech, and muscle twitching.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize circumoral paresthesia as the earliest sign of systemic local-anesthetic toxicity.

**D:** Increasing local-anesthetic levels can progress from CNS excitation and seizures to respiratory depression and arrest.

As the plasma concentration of local anesthetic increases, CNS excitation occurs, resulting in generalized seizures followed by respiratory depression and respiratory arrest.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Increasing local-anesthetic levels can progress from CNS excitation and seizures to respiratory depression and arrest.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize circumoral paresthesia as the earliest sign of systemic local-anesthetic toxicity.

**Evidence sources:**

## S03-Q240

Domain 14 · GI and hepatobiliary surgery

Which induction approaches have been used to secure the airway in an infant with pyloric stenosis?

A. Three approaches have been used: rapid-sequence induction, modified rapid-sequence induction, and inhalational induction.

B. Rapid-sequence induction is generally recommended because gastric outlet obstruction increases aspiration risk, although inhalational induction has succeeded.

C. Standard RSI has greater hypoxemia odds than modified RSI, and multiple attempts further increase those odds.

D. Cricoid pressure was used in 70%–76% of infants with either RSI or modified RSI.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: A**

**A — correct:** Three approaches have been used: rapid-sequence induction, modified rapid-sequence induction, and inhalational induction.

Three approaches have been used: rapid-sequence induction, modified rapid-sequence induction, and inhalational induction.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Select an induction strategy for an infant with pyloric stenosis.

**B:** Rapid-sequence induction is generally recommended because gastric outlet obstruction increases aspiration risk, although inhalational induction has succeeded.

An RSI is generally recommended because of the gastric outlet obstruction and the risk of aspiration, although some have performed inhalational inductions successfully.366 The risk of hypoxemia developing during an RSI in infants during induction of anesthesia is based on a retrospective review of 296 infants’ data from Boston Children’s Hospital between 2012 and 2018.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Rapid-sequence induction is generally recommended because gastric outlet obstruction increases aspiration risk, although inhalational induction has succeeded.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Select an induction strategy for an infant with pyloric stenosis.

**C:** Standard RSI has greater hypoxemia odds than modified RSI, and multiple attempts further increase those odds.

Standard RSI had an odds ratio for hypoxemia of 6.5 compared with a modified RSI (which includes mask ventilation after induction of anesthesia but before tracheal intubation).367 The odds ratio was increased to 18 for developing hypoxemia during multiple attempts at intubation compared with a single intubation attempt.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Standard RSI has greater hypoxemia odds than modified RSI, and multiple attempts further increase those odds.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Select an induction strategy for an infant with pyloric stenosis.

**D:** Cricoid pressure was used in 70%–76% of infants with either RSI or modified RSI.

Interestingly, cricoid pressure was used during induction whether using an RSI or modified RSI in 70% to 76% of infants.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Cricoid pressure was used in 70%–76% of infants with either RSI or modified RSI.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Select an induction strategy for an infant with pyloric stenosis.

**Evidence sources:**

- Cote 7e (2024).pdf — PDF p. 1549, passage 189746

## S03-Q006

Domain 1 · Airway devices and intubation

When inserting an airway exchange catheter before a potentially difficult reintubation, which technique helps prevent bronchial trauma?

A. Use a fixed insertion depth without aligning the airway exchange catheter with the in-situ tracheal tube markings.

B. Advance the airway exchange catheter beyond the depth marked by the in-situ tracheal tube before extubation.

C. Align the airway-exchange-catheter markings with the markings on the in-situ tracheal tube as the catheter is inserted.

D. Align the airway exchange catheter markings only after the tracheal tube has been removed.

## S03-Q009

Domain 1 · Extubation and complications

After a long operation with major bleeding and fluid replacement, an airway cuff leak is present, but overall extubation readiness remains uncertain. What is the best plan?

A. Proceed after confirming consciousness and command following, despite incomplete neuromuscular recovery.

B. Integrate the cuff leak with complete readiness assessment and consider delayed extubation.

C. Insert an airway-exchange catheter and proceed despite unresolved concerns about overall readiness.

D. Proceed when a cuff leak and satisfactory oxygenation are present, using these as the principal readiness criteria.

**C:** Insert an airway-exchange catheter and proceed despite unresolved concerns about overall readiness.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Insert an airway-exchange catheter and proceed despite unresolved concerns about overall readiness.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize the limited predictive value of the cuff-leak test for extubation success.

**D:** Proceed when a cuff leak and satisfactory oxygenation are present, using these as the principal readiness criteria.

The cuff-leak test has not been uniformly demonstrated to predict successful extubation.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Proceed when a cuff leak and satisfactory oxygenation are present, using these as the principal readiness criteria.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Recognize the limited predictive value of the cuff-leak test for extubation success.

## S03-Q031

A patient with carotid stenosis is scheduled for surgery. Which anesthetic planning step best protects cerebral perfusion?

## S03-Q041

An incapacitated patient needs necessary emergency care and no contrary wishes are known. Which consent principle applies?

**Answer: C**

**C — correct:** Provide necessary care under the presumption that a reasonable person would have consented.

In an emergency with no indication to the contrary, necessary care may be provided under the presumption that a reasonable person would have consented.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Apply the principle of presumptive consent to an incapacitated patient who needs necessary emergency care when no contrary indication is known.

**D:** The provider must make disclosed information accessible and understandable.

The provider is responsible for making disclosed information accessible and understandable.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “The provider must make disclosed information accessible and understandable.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply the principle of presumptive consent to an incapacitated patient who needs necessary emergency care when no contrary indication is known.

## S03-Q042

A diagnostic test has a known posttest odds value. How is diagnostic confidence calculated?

A. Posttest odds equal pretest odds multiplied by the positive likelihood ratio.

B. Pretest odds equal prevalence divided by one minus prevalence.

C. The positive likelihood ratio equals sensitivity divided by one minus specificity.

D. Posttest odds divided by one plus posttest odds, multiplied by 100%.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: D**

Posttest odds equal pretest odds multiplied by the positive likelihood ratio.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Posttest odds equal pretest odds multiplied by the positive likelihood ratio.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply the diagnostic-confidence equation that converts posttest odds into the probability that a positive test represents the condition.

**B:** Pretest odds equal prevalence divided by one minus prevalence.

Pretest odds are calculated as prevalence divided by one minus prevalence.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Pretest odds equal prevalence divided by one minus prevalence.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply the diagnostic-confidence equation that converts posttest odds into the probability that a positive test represents the condition.

**C:** The positive likelihood ratio equals sensitivity divided by one minus specificity.

The positive likelihood ratio is sensitivity divided by one minus specificity.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “The positive likelihood ratio equals sensitivity divided by one minus specificity.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply the diagnostic-confidence equation that converts posttest odds into the probability that a positive test represents the condition.

**D — correct:** Posttest odds divided by one plus posttest odds, multiplied by 100%.

Diagnostic confidence is calculated as posttest odds divided by one plus posttest odds, multiplied by 100%. 

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Apply the diagnostic-confidence equation that converts posttest odds into the probability that a positive test represents the condition.

**Evidence sources:**

- Bonica’s Management of Pain 5e (2019).pdf — PDF p. 4900, passage 262290

## S03-Q043

Attrition bias is systematic difference in withdrawal of patients from the two intervention groups.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Attrition bias is a systematic difference in withdrawal between intervention groups.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Distinguish detection bias from selection, performance, and attrition bias when appraising trial evidence.

**D:** Selection bias is a systematic difference between patients receiving each intervention.

Selection bias is systematic difference between the patients receiving each intervention.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Selection bias is a systematic difference between patients receiving each intervention.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Distinguish detection bias from selection, performance, and attrition bias when appraising trial evidence.

## S03-Q081

Domain 5 · Workstations circuits and ventilators

When oxygen supply pressure decreases, what does the oxygen-failure cutoff valve do to the nitrous-oxide supply?

A. The oxygen failure cutoff valve shuts off or proportionally decreases nitrous oxide when oxygen supply pressure decreases.

B. A high-priority alarm is actuated when decreasing oxygen supply pressure reaches a predetermined threshold such as 30 psig.

C. The valve serves as an interface between the oxygen and nitrous oxide supply sources.

D. The oxygen failure cutoff valve is located downstream from the nitrous oxide supply source.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: A**

**A — correct:** The oxygen failure cutoff valve shuts off or proportionally decreases nitrous oxide when oxygen supply pressure decreases.

The valve shuts off or proportionally decreases nitrous-oxide delivery as oxygen supply pressure falls; this directly answers the requested valve response.

**B:** A high-priority alarm is actuated when decreasing oxygen supply pressure reaches a predetermined threshold such as 30 psig.

The low-oxygen-supply alarm is a separate warning function. It does not describe how the cutoff valve changes nitrous-oxide delivery.

**C:** The valve serves as an interface between the oxygen and nitrous oxide supply sources.

Serving as an interface describes the valve’s location within the gas-supply relationship, not its response to falling oxygen pressure.

**D:** The oxygen failure cutoff valve is located downstream from the nitrous oxide supply source.

Being downstream from the nitrous-oxide source describes valve placement, not the functional response requested.

**Evidence sources:**

- Barash 9e (2023).pdf — PDF p. 1932, passage 246570

## S03-Q135

An infant with pyloric stenosis is being optimized before pyloromyotomy. Which finding establishes readiness to proceed?

A. Plasma pH has just normalized, but residual alkalosis and delayed cerebrospinal-fluid equilibration remain concerns.

**C — correct:** Dehydration and the hypochloremic metabolic alkalosis have been corrected.

Pyloromyotomy is not a surgical emergency; dehydration and metabolic imbalance should be corrected before surgery.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Prepare an infant with pyloric stenosis by correcting dehydration and metabolic abnormalities before anesthesia.

**D:** A nasogastric tube remains in place despite persistent electrolyte abnormalities.

Nasogastric tubes are not always used preoperatively because they may worsen the electrolyte imbalance.

## S03-Q163

Domain 10 · Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

What does the term pharmacogenetics describe?

A. Pharmacogenomics encompasses all genes that may determine drug responses.

B. Different mutations may be silent or change protein structure as nonsense or missense variants.

C. The role of genetic variation in drug response or adverse reactions.

D. A single-nucleotide polymorphism is the most common allelic variation.

**Difficulty: Intermediate**

**Answer: C**

**A:** Pharmacogenomics encompasses all genes that may determine drug responses.

A broader term, “pharmacogenomics,” was introduced in the 1990s with emergence of the Human Genome Project and the development of the genome sciences, encompassing all genes in the genome that may determine drug responses.12 We now know that the human genome contains about 21,000 genes.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Pharmacogenomics encompasses all genes that may determine drug responses.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Perioperative Medicine and Medical Subspecialties / Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

**B:** Different mutations may be silent or change protein structure as nonsense or missense variants.

However, since more than one codon (triplet of nucleotides) code for the same amino acid, not all mutations cause structural changes in the protein (called nonsense variant), whereas others do change the structure of the protein (called missense variant).

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Different mutations may be silent or change protein structure as nonsense or missense variants.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Perioperative Medicine and Medical Subspecialties / Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

**C — correct:** The role of genetic variation in drug response or adverse reactions.

The key clinical principle is that barbiturates in patients with porphyria (1937),9 cholinesterase deficiency leading to succinylcholine-induced prolonged apnea (1957),10 and malignant hyperthermia (1962).11 Basic Concepts and Nomenclature The term “pharmacogenetics,” which was coined by Friedrich Vogel of Heidelberg, Germany, in 1959,11 refers to the role of genetic variation affecting drug response or adverse reactions to drugs.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Perioperative Medicine and Medical Subspecialties / Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

**D:** A single-nucleotide polymorphism is the most common allelic variation.

The most common type of allelic variation is a single-nucleotide polymorphism (SNP) when two alternative bases occur at an appreciable frequency (.1% of the population).13 On the other hand, mutations which occur less frequently (,1% of the population) include duplications, deletions, insertions, translocation, or inversion of DNA segments.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “A single-nucleotide polymorphism is the most common allelic variation.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Perioperative Medicine and Medical Subspecialties / Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

**Evidence sources:**

- Cote 7e (2024).pdf — PDF p. 141, passage 183122

## S03-Q164

B. Atropine 0.01–0.02 mg/kg IV should precede succinylcholine to reduce arrhythmias.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Hyperkalemic arrests were reported mainly in children with undiagnosed Duchenne muscular dystrophy.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Perioperative Medicine and Medical Subspecialties / Neuromuscular genetic and uncommon disease

**D — correct:** 1.0 mg/kg IV.

## S03-Q204

Domain 12 · Local anesthetic application and LAST

During local-anesthetic systemic toxicity, airway and seizure support have begun. Which specific therapy is accepted as first-line and should not be delayed?

A. Administer vasopressin.

B. Administer a calcium-channel blocker.

C. Use propofol emulsion as a substitute for lipid emulsion.

D. Administer intravenous lipid emulsion.

**Difficulty: Foundational**

**Answer: D**

**A:** Administer vasopressin.

Vasopressin is specifically not recommended during resuscitation from local-anesthetic systemic toxicity.

**B:** Administer a calcium-channel blocker.

Calcium-channel blockers should be avoided during local-anesthetic systemic-toxicity resuscitation.

**C:** Use propofol emulsion as a substitute for lipid emulsion.

The cited source distinguishes lipid emulsion from propofol; propofol is not the accepted lipid-emulsion substitute.

**D — correct:** Administer intravenous lipid emulsion.

Intravenous lipid emulsion is accepted as first-line specific therapy for LAST and should not be delayed while airway and seizure support are provided.

## S03-Q222

During one-lung ventilation for pneumonectomy, which tidal-volume strategy is associated with greater postoperative respiratory-failure risk?

B. A tidal volume of 5 mL/kg during OLV reduces the inflammatory response to alveolar cytokines.

**Answer: A**

The key clinical principle is that 1552 SECTION IV • Adult Subspecialty Management pneumonectomy patients showed that patients that received median tidal volumes greater than 8 mL/kg had a greater risk of respiratory failure in the postoperative period after pneumonectomy.

Best-answer reasoning: This option directly satisfies the tested objective: Apply lung-protective tidal-volume principles during one-lung ventilation after pneumonectomy.

**B:** A tidal volume of 5 mL/kg during OLV reduces the inflammatory response to alveolar cytokines.

**C:** A prudent approach is 5–6 mL/kg ideal body weight with peak and plateau pressures limited during OLV.

Considering these factors, it is prudent to use lower tidal volumes (i.e., 5–6 mL/kg, ideal body weight) in the pneumonectomy patient and limit peak and plateau inspiratory pressures (i.e., <35 and 25 cmH2O) during OLV.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “A prudent approach is 5–6 mL/kg ideal body weight with peak and plateau pressures limited during OLV.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply lung-protective tidal-volume principles during one-lung ventilation after pneumonectomy.

**D:** Tidal volumes less than 6 mL/kg were associated with lower postoperative respiratory-failure risk.

In contrast, patients that received tidal volumes less than 6 mL/kg were at lower risk of respiratory failure.

Why it is not best here: This option addresses “Tidal volumes less than 6 mL/kg were associated with lower postoperative respiratory-failure risk.” rather than satisfying the complete tested objective: Apply lung-protective tidal-volume principles during one-lung ventilation after pneumonectomy.
